# 1.6.0 FILE VERDICTS — EVERY `.rs` FILE IN THE WORKSPACE

**Denominator pinned at `27c21946428ec7dca2f177844332577b129b21fb` (branch `consolidated/1.6.0`).**

```sh
git -C /Users/matthew/Developer/GetBusbar/busbar ls-files '*.rs' | wc -l   # 1802
```

**1802 tracked `.rs` files in, 1802 verdict rows out.** Every path in that listing appears exactly once
in the table below. The arithmetic is at the end and it is checked by the generator, not asserted.

### THE TREE MOVED UNDER THIS SWEEP, AND THE DENOMINATOR DID NOT

This checkout is shared. `HEAD` was `27c21946428ec7dca2f177844332577b129b21fb` when the file list was
pinned and `aa0b4f5badb9885f804c8b2077ddf523796d898b` when the sweep finished — somebody else landed
commits while this ran. **`git ls-files '*.rs'` was re-run at the end and `diff`ed against the pinned
list: byte-identical, 1802 both times. No `.rs` file appeared and none vanished.** The verdicts below
are therefore about the same 1802 files they claim to be about, even though the commits around them changed.

## THE VERDICT SET, AND WHY IT IS NOT THE KERNEL SLICE'S VOCABULARY

`docs/design/1.6.0-file-verdicts-kernel.md` (398 files, `crates/busbar-kernel/` only) was read first and its
method is carried forward — module-declaration graph including `#[path]`, symbol-level production-caller
probes with tests excluded, and "where a source scan is not enough, the logic was read". **Its verdict
vocabulary is deliberately not reused**, and this is the one place I diverge, so it is stated plainly:

| kernel slice | this sweep |
|---|---|
| `TRASH` / `RECODE` / `FIX` / `KEEP` / `UNCLASSIFIED` | `LIVE` / `TOOLING` / `TEST` / `HALF-MIGRATED` / `ORPHAN` / `DUPLICATE` / `UNCLEAR` |
| answers **what should be done to the file** | answers **what the file is and whether anything reaches it** |

The two are orthogonal, not rival: a `KEEP` there can be an `ORPHAN` here (nothing is wrong with the code;
nothing calls it), and a `FIX` there is usually `LIVE` here (it is reached, and what it does when reached is
wrong). Both of the kernel slice's orphans are reproduced below **independently**, by two oracles that
never read that document — which is the strongest thing I can say for either sweep.

## HOW REACHABILITY WAS MEASURED — TWO INDEPENDENT ORACLES THAT HAD TO AGREE

**Oracle A — cargo's own dep-info.** `target/{debug,release}` holds **3086 `.d` files**, and a `.d` file lists
every source file rustc actually compiled into that unit. This is not an approximation of "does it compile";
it is the compiler's own record. **1719 of 1802 files are witnessed compiled.**

**Oracle B — a module-declaration graph** built from every package's real targets (lib, every `[[bin]]`,
`src/bin/*`, `build.rs`, `tests/*.rs`, `benches/*`, `examples/*`, `fuzz_targets/*` — **226 roots**), resolving
every `mod x;` including `#[path]` and `include!`. **1790 of 1802 reached.**

**They were forced to agree.** The first run of Oracle B reported **463 unreached files**. That was a bug in
me, not a finding in the tree: I had resolved `#[path = "tests/x.rs"]` in a non-`mod.rs` file relative to
`dir/filestem/` instead of relative to the file's own directory. Oracle A is what exposed it — 425 files
cargo had demonstrably compiled were sitting in my "unreached" list. After the fix, **every file Oracle A
witnessed compiled is reached by Oracle B (the set difference is 0)**, and the residue is 12 files:

| residue | what it is |
|---|--:|
| `xtask/fixtures/**` — 10 files | gate self-test fixture trees, deliberately never compiled (they are *inputs* to gates, not Rust the workspace builds) |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/handlers/tests/chat_tests.rs` | **ORPHAN** |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/tests/civil_tests.rs` | **ORPHAN** |

Those last two are exactly the two files the kernel slice names, found here without reference to it.

## HOW I CONTROLLED EVERY ZERO

A false zero and a real zero print the same thing, so no zero below is reported un-controlled.

1. **Every probe was run against known-LIVE files in the same invocation.** The reference probe returns
   `teller.rs` 82, `root/kernel.rs` 49, `cost.rs` 130, `root/adapters.rs` 6 production path-references. A probe
   that returns 0 for everything cannot return those numbers.
2. **The crate-usage probe was controlled the same way**: `busbar-kernel` 1485 production references. Not a
   zero machine.
3. **The duplicate detector was controlled against the instance handed to me.** Run blind over 1802 files it
   returned `fn tap_join_verdict: busbar-a2a, busbar-mcp` at `crates/busbar-a2a/src/a2a/receive.rs` and
   `crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/method.rs` — the two cited files, found independently.
4. **`docs/` is excluded from every reachability search.** Only `crates/`, `xtask/`, `fuzz/`, `examples/`,
   `testing/` are corpus. Separately, **comments are stripped before any symbol is counted** — a 22.1 MB
   comment-stripped corpus is what every probe reads. A symbol named in prose is not a symbol being used,
   and in this tree the prose is voluminous enough that this is not a technicality: the raw-text probe scored
   `vocabulary` at 25 references; against stripped source in path position it is **0**.
5. **Traps that actually fired during this sweep, and were caught:**
   - `for s in $syms` in zsh **did not word-split** a newline-separated list — the loop ran once on the whole
     blob and printed one fused count. Re-run with `while IFS= read -r`. (This is the named trap; it fired.)
   - `grep -rn --include=*.rs` — zsh glob-expanded `--include=*.rs` before `grep` saw it and the command died
     with `no matches found`, exit 1, **zero output**. Switched to `git grep -- 'path/**/*.rs'` throughout.
   - A backtick inside a double-quoted `echo` ran `Engine` as a command; caught because the output said
     `command not found` instead of printing a count.
6. **Two near-miss false ORPHANs, caught by reading rather than grepping.** `openai_chat/reader.rs` (1382
   lines) scored 0 path-references. It has **no `pub` items at all** — it is `impl ProtocolReader for
   OpenAiReader`, reachable through the trait, needing no path reference. Six `reader.rs` files,
   `in_memory/availability.rs`, `service_operations.rs`, `slots_through.rs` and `refusal_client.rs` are all
   this shape. **All are LIVE.** Had I trusted the zero, this sweep would have reported nine false orphans.
7. **One near-miss in the other direction.** `reframe.rs` scored 15 references on `prefers_event_stream` —
   which looked live until I read them: they are a *different* `prefers_event_stream` in
   `busbar-mcp/src/mcp/sse.rs`. Same for `Engine` (49 hits, a different `Engine`). Name collisions were
   resolved by opening the file, never by counting.

## THE FINDINGS

**10 ORPHAN · 15 HALF-MIGRATED · 38 DUPLICATE.** Every one is a row on the owner's TODO list.
Nothing below was deleted, moved or merged.

### ⛔ ORPHAN — 10 files, each verified by symbol and by reading

| path | money? | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/api/src/usage_migration.rs` | **YES — PARK** | All 7 top-level symbols have 0 production references. The only references anywhere are `crates/api/src/tests/usage_migration_tests.rs` (its own test) and `xtask/src/gates/no_float_money.rs:474`, which names the FILE in an allowlist rather than calling it. Nothing invokes `fold_v1_ledger`, so the one-shot usage-ledger migration never runs on a real store. |
| `crates/busbar-a2a/src/a2a/inbound_jws.rs` | no | `install_inbound_card_jws` has exactly one caller and it is `a2a/tests/inbound_jws_tests.rs:97`. The `INBOUND_JWS` OnceLock is never set in production, so `inbound_card_jws()` always returns `None` and inbound agent-card JWS verification never runs. |
| `crates/busbar-kernel-breaker/src/clock.rs` | no | Its only item `unix_time_secs` has 0 non-comment references outside the file. Its doc says it is kept "only so the root has one spelling" of the value — and the root does not use it. |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/egress/interface.rs` | no | Declares `pub trait EgressEngine`, `pub struct Engine`, `impl EgressEngine for Engine`. `EgressEngine` occurs exactly once outside this file and that once is a COMMENT (`egress/mod.rs:32`). The 49 hits on `Engine` are a different `Engine` — a name collision I checked rather than counted. |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/engine_facade.rs` | no | 8 `pub use` re-exports; the only mention of `engine_facade` in the whole tree outside itself is its own `pub mod engine_facade;` at `crates/busbar-kernel/src/lib.rs:233`. Its doc says it is the surface a plane reaches down through "once it lives in its own plane crate" — the #39 plane extraction never arrived, so the facade has no caller. |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/handlers/tests/chat_tests.rs` | no | No `mod` declaration anywhere reaches it and cargo never compiled it (absent from all 3086 dep-info files). Both independent oracles agree. |
| `crates/busbar-kernel/src/tests/civil_tests.rs` | no | Same: undeclared and never compiled. The copy `busbar-contract/src/civil.rs` declares is a placeholder with zero `#[test]`, so the contract's civil-date math ships with no executed assertions. |
| `crates/busbar-transport-sse/src/reframe.rs` | no | Every symbol it defines is referenced only here and in its own tests. `install_sse_reframe` has exactly one caller: `src/tests/reframe_tests.rs:90`. The `REFRAMER` OnceLock is never set in production, so `sse_reframe()` always returns `None`. The 15 `prefers_event_stream` hits are a DIFFERENT function in `busbar-mcp/src/mcp/sse.rs`. |
| `crates/busbar/src/root/money_book.rs` | **YES — PARK** | `reachability:root-module` RED. My independent BFS from `fn main()` over the binary crate reaches 21 of 24 production modules and NOT this one; path-position probe = 0 production references (the single hit is `xtask/src/gates/reachability.rs`, the gate naming it). `PassThroughBook` is constructed only in `root/tests/money_book.rs`. The file's own doc: "The seam is DORMANT: nothing on the serving path routes through it." |
| `crates/busbar/src/root/vocabulary.rs` | no | `reachability:root-module` RED. Independent BFS from `fn main()`: not reached. Path-position probe = 0 production references, 9 test. Unlike money_book its doc reads as live infrastructure, which is why only a measurement catches it. |

**THREE of these ten are one class, and the class is the finding.** A `OnceLock` seam whose `install_*`
function has no production caller: the lock is never set, the reader always answers `None`, and the capability
the seam exists to carry is silently absent while every one of its own tests passes. I probed **every**
`install_*` function in the workspace for a production caller — 25 of 28 have one, and these three do not:

| file (ORPHAN) | seam | installer's only caller | what is therefore never true at runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| `crates/busbar-transport-sse/src/reframe.rs` | `install_sse_reframe` / `PassThroughReframe` | `src/tests/reframe_tests.rs:90` | `sse_reframe()` returns `None` forever |
| `crates/busbar-a2a/src/a2a/inbound_jws.rs` | `install_inbound_card_jws` / `PassThroughInboundJws` | `a2a/tests/inbound_jws_tests.rs:97` | `inbound_card_jws()` returns `None` forever — **inbound agent-card JWS verification never runs** |
| `crates/busbar/src/root/money_book.rs` | `PassThroughBook` | `root/tests/money_book.rs` only | the money-book seam is never on the serving path (**PARK P2**) |

**The control that makes this a finding and not a design.** The same DECISION #26 wave produced a FOURTH
pass-through seam, `PassThroughEgressTrust`, and that one **is** installed in production —
`crates/busbar/src/main.rs:679`. Four seams were built to the same shape; one was wired. If none were wired I
would be reporting a wave that has not landed yet. One being wired is what makes the other three an omission.

### ⚠ HALF-MIGRATED — 15 files

Spec **DECISION #19** is the authority: *"busbar-core dissolves per #36/#37 into `busbar-kernel` (loop) + the 8
`busbar-kernel-<name>` workflow crates + `busbar-core-{admin,oauth2,substrate}`"*, with **#36** naming the 8 and
**#37**/**#39** naming the end state. A file is HALF-MIGRATED here when **some of its top-level symbols already
exist at the spec-named new home and some do not**.

**The threshold is stated so it can be argued with:** a file qualifies at **≥25% of its top-level symbols shared
with the new home, or ≥4 shared symbols**. Below that I do not call it a finding, because single shared names
like `settle`, `walk`, `Outcome`, `Entry` and `Direction` are ordinary collisions, not evidence of a move. That

guard**. The fix went to the fork that is not serving. That is what half-migrated costs.

### ⧉ DUPLICATE — 38 files

Two rules, both mechanical, both stated:

* **whole-file** — comment- and whitespace-normalised content identical to another file; or **≥30% of a file's
  normalised bytes** are top-level declarations that exist byte-identically elsewhere. A file with two shared
  helpers out of 1382 lines is *not* a duplicated file, and is left LIVE with a note (that rule change alone
  moved 35 files off this list, including both `*/reader.rs` and `busbar-transport-http/src/lib.rs`).
* **concern-level** — the same top-level name declared in two fat plane crates, which is
  `structure-lint:plane-dup`'s own rule. Eight files qualify and they are listed first.

## 💰 THE MONEY PARKS — never self-approved, cell + root cause + recommendation

Money is binary. Each of these is a finding on a path that prices, meters or migrates a billed quantity, so
each is PARKED for the owner rather than judged here.

| # | cell | root cause | recommendation |
|--:|---|---|---|
| P1 | `crates/api/src/usage_migration.rs` — all 7 top-level symbols, 0 production callers | The one-shot pre-M1b→`usage_units` usage-ledger migration is written, tested and **never invoked**. The only non-test mention in the tree is `xtask/src/gates/no_float_money.rs:474`, which names the FILE in an allowlist — it does not call it. So on any store carrying pre-M1b rows, `USAGE_SCHEMA_V2` is never stamped and the scalar `TierTokens` rows are never folded onto the name-keyed ledger. | Owner decides whether 1.6.0 ships the migration or drops it. If it ships, `fold_v1_ledger` needs a caller on the store-open path and a proof that a v1 store round-trips. Do not let a migration that no code runs count as a migration that ran. |
| P2 | `crates/busbar/src/root/money_book.rs` — `PassThroughBook`, constructed only in `root/tests/money_book.rs` | `reachability:root-module` is RED on it and the file's own doc concedes it: *"The seam is DORMANT: nothing on the serving path routes through it."* The W3 fold it exists to enable (rates+usage+ledger → one `busbar-kernel-ledger`) has not happened, so the seam is a money interface with no traffic. | Either route settlement through the seam and delete the three direct paths, or delete the seam. A dormant money seam is worse than none: it reads to the next agent as though the fold already landed. |
| P3 | `crates/busbar-kernel-budget/src/window.rs` — 61% of its normalised bytes | `window_end`, `civil_from_days` and `days_from_civil` are **byte-identical** to the copies in `crates/busbar-kernel/src/governance/mod.rs`. Budget-window boundaries decide which window a charge lands in. Two copies of a window boundary is two answers to "was this spend inside the budget period". | Fold to one home (`busbar-kernel-ledger` per #36) and make the other a re-export. Until then any fix to one is a silent divergence. |
| P4 | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs` — 9 of 15 top-level symbols already in `busbar-kernel-ledger` | Half-migrated rate-card resolution. The kernel copy is LIVE; the ledger copy is where #36 says the book belongs. The kernel slice records that `busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs:40-47` carries a `u64::MAX` clamp the live `cost.rs:138-145` does not (M1/M31). | Same shape as P3 and the same danger as the breaker: the hardening landed on the copy that is not serving. Owner picks the survivor; the survivor must be the one on the enforcement door. |
| P5 | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/governance/mod.rs` — 14 of 60 top-level symbols already at the new home | Half-migrated enforcement/usage state, and the other end of P3's duplicate. `try_admit` is the afford gate. | Fold per #36 (`busbar-kernel-budget` = the afford gate). |

**`crates/busbar-mcp/src/mcp/method.rs` is money-adjacent and is NOT parked here**, deliberately: its finding is
the `tap_join_verdict` duplicate and an 8/63 half-migration, neither of which moves a billed quantity by itself.
It is a DUPLICATE row, not a park. Saying so is the point — a park that is not about money dilutes the ones that are.

## UNCLEAR — 0 files, and that is a claim rather than a default

Every one of the 1802 was resolved by at least one of: cargo's own dep-info (1719), the 226-root module graph
(1790), a symbol-level production-caller probe over the comment-stripped corpus, a crate-level link probe from
`cargo metadata` rooted at the `busbar` binary, or — wherever a scan was not enough — opening the file and
reading it. **Where reading was what settled it, the row says so.**

**The residual risk, stated plainly.** A `LIVE` verdict here means *production-compiled and reached from the
composition root by module path or trait impl*. It does **not** mean the file was read line by line — 1802 files
were not read. `LIVE` is a reachability result at the strength of the instruments above; the ORPHAN,
HALF-MIGRATED and DUPLICATE rows are the ones with evidence behind them.
